Annual Securities Report

 From April 1, 2024 to March 31, 2025
       (The 113th Fiscal Year)




       Eisai Co., Ltd.
                                                                                        Translation for reference purpose only

As used in this document, references to the “the Company,” “Eisai,” “we,” “us” and “our” are to Eisai Co., Ltd. and, except as the
context otherwise requires, its consolidated subsidiaries.
This document is English summary version of the annual securities report in Japanese which the Company submitted to Director of the
Kanto Local Finance Bureau. In the event of any inconsistency between the documents in the two versions, the statements in the
Japanese version shall prevail.
This document is not reviewed by the Accounting Auditor.
The annual securities report (Japanese version) can be viewed on the company’s website;
https://www.eisai.co.jp/ir/library/securities/index.html

2. History
      The Company was founded in November 1936 by Toyoji Naito, then Managing Director of Tanabe Motosaburo Store Co.,
    Ltd., who established the “Sakuragaoka Laboratory Co., Ltd” in Mikawashima, Arakawa-ku, Tokyo. Five years later, in
    December 1941, Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. was established in Honjo-machi (currently Honjo City), Saitama Prefecture, with a
    capital of 180,000 yen.

    June 1942           Opened the Honjo Plant (name at that time) in Honjo-machi (currently Honjo City), Saitama Prefecture
    December 1944       Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. merged with Sakuragaoka Laboratory Co., Ltd. and the surviving company is
                        launched as Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. The head office is located in Takehaya-cho, Koishikawa-ku, Tokyo
                        (currently Koishikawa, Bunkyo ku, Tokyo)
    May 1955            The company name is changed from Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. to Eisai Co., Ltd., as it is known today.
    September 1961      Listed on the First Section of the Tokyo Stock Exchange
    July 1965           Participated in the management of Sansho Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. (company name later changed to
                        Sannova Co., Ltd.)
    March 1966          Kawashima Plant (currently Kawashima Industrial Park) is opened in Kawashima-cho, Gifu Prefecture
                        (currently Kakamigahara City)
    September 1979      Asian holding company (Eisai Asia Regional Services Pte. Ltd.) is established in Singapore
    November 1981       Misato Plant opens in Misato-mura, Saitama Prefecture (currently Misato Town)
    January 1982        The Tsukuba Research Laboratories open in Toyosato-cho (currently Tsukuba City), Ibaraki Prefecture
    October 1983        Eisai Chemical Co., Ltd. (currently Kashima Plant) is established in Hasaki-machi (now Kamisu City),
                        Ibaraki Prefecture
    November 1987       Eisai Research Institute of Boston, Inc. (name at that time) is established in the U.S.
    September 1989      Eisai Deutschland GmbH (currently Eisai GmbH) is established in Germany
    August 1990         Eisai London Research Laboratories, Ltd. (currently Eisai Ltd.) is established in the U.K.
    October             Sanko Junyaku Co., Ltd. (later renamed EIDIA Co., Ltd.) signs a business alliance agreement in the
                        diagnostics products business
    April 1992          U.S. holding company (Eisai Corporation of North America) is established in the U.S.
    February 1995       Eisai Pharmatechnology, Inc. (name at that time) is established in the U.S.
    April               Eisai Inc. is established in the U.S.
    October             Eisai Ltd. is established in the U.K.
    January 1996        Eisai S.A. (Currently Eisai S.A.S.) is established in France
    March               Eisai Suzhou Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. (currently Eisai China Inc.) is established in China
    April               Elmed Eisai Co., Ltd. is established
    April 1997          KAN Research Institute, Inc. is established (later merged into the Company)
    April               Eisai Korea Inc. is established in South Korea
    June 2002           Eisai Medical Research Inc. (currently Eisai Inc.) is established in the U.S.
    June 2004           Company adopts the “Company with Committees governance system”
    October             European regional headquarters and holding company (Eisai Europe Ltd.) is established in the U.K.
    March 2007          Eisai Manufacturing Ltd. is established in the U.K.
    March               Eisai Pharmatechnology & Manufacturing Pvt. Ltd.（currently Eisai Pharmaceuticals India Pvt. Ltd.) is
                        established in India
    April               Acquired Morphotek, Inc. in the U.S. (later merged into Eisai Inc.)
    October             Sanko Junyaku Co., Ltd. (later renamed EIDIA Co., Ltd.) becomes a wholly owned subsidiary through an
                        exchange of shares
    January 2008        Acquired MGI Pharma Inc. in the U.S. (later merged into Eisai Inc.)
    December 2010       H3Biomedicine Inc. is established in the U.S. (later merged into Eisai Inc.)
    March 2014          Misato Plant transferred to Bushu Pharmaceuticals Ltd. (Saitama Prefecture)
    November            China regional headquarters and holding company (Eisai China Holdings Ltd) is established in China
    December 2015       EIDIA Co., Ltd. is sold to Sekisui Chemical Co., Ltd. (Osaka Prefecture)
    April 2016          A portion of the Company’s business in the field of gastrointestinal diseases in Japan is split off and a new
                        integrated company, EA Pharma Co., Ltd., is established by means of an absorption-type split with
                        Ajinomoto Pharmaceuticals Co., Ltd.
    April               Sannova Co., Ltd. is sold to Alfresa Holdings Corporation (Tokyo)
    April 2019          Elmed Eisai Co., Ltd. is sold to Nichi-Iko Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. (Toyama Prefecture)

July         Eisai Center for Genetics Guided Dementia Discovery (G2D2), a discovery laboratory, is established in the
             U.S. and begins full-scale operations
April 2022   Moved from the First Section of the Tokyo Stock Exchange to the Prime Market due to the reclassification
             of the Tokyo Stock Exchange market
April 2024   KAN Research Institute, Inc. (Hyogo Prefecture) is absorbed into the Company by merger
May 2025     EcoNaviSta, Inc. (Tokyo) becomes a consolidated subsidiary of the Company through a TOB

3. Description of the Business
      The Group consists of the consolidated financial report submitting company (the Company), 48 consolidated subsidiaries
    and 1 associated company accounted for using the equity method. Its operations are classified into the pharmaceuticals
    business and other business. The pharmaceutical business conducts research and development, manufacturing, and sales of
    prescription medicines and OTC products and others. The pharmaceutical business is organized into the following five
    reporting segments in this report: Japan, Americas (North America), China, EMEA (Europe, the Middle East, Africa, Russia
    and Oceania), and East Asia Global South (primarily South Korea, Taiwan, India, ASEAN, and Central and South America).
    As the Asia and Latin America pharmaceutical business includes Asia (excluding Japan and China), Central and South
    America, and South Africa, the name was changed to East Asia Global South pharmaceutical business starting from October 1,
    2024.


      The relationships between businesses segments, main products, and main companies are as follows.

      The Eisai Labor Union and the Eisai Headquarters Labor Union were established respectively as unit unions for the Honjo

    Eisai Labor Union.

II. Status of the Business
 1. Management Policy, Management Environment and Issues that Need to be Addressed
     Statements in the text regarding the future are based on the judgment of the Group as of the end of the current consolidated
   fiscal year.


   (i) Corporate Concept
        The Group defines its corporate concept as “to give first thought to patients and the people in the daily living domain, and to
     increase the benefits that health care provides to them.” Guided by this concept, all directors, corporate officers and employees
     aspire to meet the various needs of global health care as representatives of a “human health care (hhc) company” that is
     capable of making a meaningful contribution under any health care system. The Group’s mission is to increase the satisfaction
     of patients and the people in the daily living domain, and to empower them to realize their fullest life through an hhc
     ecosystem based on collaboration with other industries and groups. The Group believes that revenues and earnings will be
     generated by fulfilling this mission. The Group places importance on this sequence of placing the mission before the ensuing
     results.
        Translating this hhc concept into action, the Group is committed to deepening the relationships built on trust with its
     principal stakeholders, namely patients and customers, shareholders, and employees, while continuously ensuring compliance
     with applicable laws and ethical standards, thereby enhancing corporate value. The Company codified this corporate concept
     into its Articles of Incorporation and endeavors to share its basic concept with shareholders.
        Based on the hhc concept, the Group seeks to increase long-term corporate value by creating social impact through efficient
     realization of social good in the form of relieving anxiety over health and reducing health disparities.


   (ii) Eisai’s Future Creation Strategy
         In March 2025, based on the hhc concept, the Group formulated the “Eisai Future Creation Strategy” to create a world where
     everyone can realize their fullest life. By placing this strategy at the core of management, the Group aims to contribute to
     patients and the people in the daily living domain through our pharmaceutical business and other operations, while supporting
     this with strengthened corporate governance and long-term efforts toward environmental conservation and solving social
     issues. We strive to achieve continuous corporate growth and contribute to the sustainable development of society. The Group
     will set important issues and targets to be addressed as a priority in our medium-term business plan for the medium- to long-
     term realization of this strategy, and will implement initiatives to achieve them.


   (iii) Management Environment, Management Policies and Strategies, and Priority Business and Financial Issues to be Addressed
        The Group launched “EWAY Future & Beyond,” a medium-term business plan, in April 2021.


     1) Medium-Term Business Plan “EWAY Future & Beyond”
         In “EWAY Future & Beyond”, the first five years starting in FY 2021 are “EWAY Future”, while FY 2026 onward is
        “EWAY Beyond”. In addition, the most important stakeholders to whom the Group contributes were expanded from
        “patients and their families” to “patients and the people in the daily living domain”. In line with our desire to empower
        patients and the people in the daily living domain to “realize their fullest life,” we aim to evolve into an hhceco (hhc concept
        + ecosystem) company by creating solutions based on science and data in the neurology and oncology fields, where we have
        our greatest strength and unmet medical needs are extremely high, through an ecosystem developed in collaboration with
        other industries.
          In 2023, we formulated new materiality to effectively realize social good through the hhc ecosystem. In addition to
        achieving social good in the areas of dementia, oncology and global health, we have identified maximization of human
        resource value and financial strategies as important material issues, and set and identified long-term goals, KPIs and risks for
        fiscal 2030. With this materiality as our compass, we will work to effectively realize social good.


     2) Major Progress and Initiatives under Medium-Term Business Plan “EWAY Future & Beyond”
           Under the Deep Human Biology Learning (DHBL) drug discovery and development structure, which implements drug
        discovery research by maximizing the use of human biology evidence accumulated internally through profiling of various
        biomarkers to understand disease pathology and view diseases as a Disease Continuum, we are promoting drug discovery
        and development, from the establishment and validation of drug discovery hypotheses to obtaining regulatory approval,
        focusing on the neurological field, primarily dementia represented by Alzheimer’s disease (AD), and the oncology field,
        primarily refractory cancers, in which the Group can most quickly and deeply access the relevant human biology. We seek to




                                                                - 14 -
                                                                                Translation for reference purpose only

make ongoing contributions in the area of global health as well.
  We also aim to provide value by building an ecosystem that supports people at all stages of life, from the daily living to
the medical domain, in collaboration with partners such as academia, companies and local governments. Additionally, to
support these value creation efforts, we are also working on restructuring to pursue efficiencies and raise profitability. We
are optimizing global operations and transforming the company-wide profit structure by fundamentally reviewing the
organization and processes, rather than simply cutting costs.


(a) Dementia Area
    Lecanemab (brand name: Leqembi) has been approved for the treatment of early AD in 44 countries and regions
  including the United States, Japan, China, and countries and regions in Europe and Asia, and applications for approval
  have been submitted in a further 12. AD is a progressive and fatal disease that requires early diagnosis and treatment. As a
  pioneer in the field of AD in the U.S., Japan, and China, where we have already launched this product, we are working to
  build, improve, and streamline consecutive pathways for diagnosis and treatment that move from cognitive function
  testing to APOE4 testing, amyloid-β (Aβ) testing (PET: positron-emission tomography, CSF: cerebrospinal fluid testing),
  administration, and ARIA (amyloid-related imaging abnormalities) monitoring. Specifically, we are working toward
  approvals not only in the first destination the United States but also in Japan and other countries for intravenous infusion
  maintenance dosing that enabled dosage regimens of once every 4 weeks after completing the initial dosage of once every
  2 weeks, and subcutaneous injections with auto injectors (SC-AI) that enable dosage either at home or in the facility. We
  are also making steady progress on expanding pre-screenings and implementing definitive diagnosis for Aβ accumulation
  using blood biomarkers. These collaborative efforts with multiple partners will be ongoing.
    Other development projects based on the AD continuum are also in progress. AHEAD 3-45, a Phase III clinical study
  evaluating lecanemab for preclinical (asymptomatic) AD has completed patient enrollment and is progressing smoothly
  toward obtaining top-line results by FY2028. The anti-microtubule binding region (MTBR) tau antibody E2814 is being
  evaluated in combination with lecanemab in the Tau NexGen Study (Phase II / III), conducted by the Dominantly
  Inherited Alzheimer Network Trials Unit (DIAN-TU) for dominant hereditary AD, and a Phase II clinical study of E2814
  targeting sporadic AD was also initiated in 2024. Furthermore, E2511, the selective Tropomyosin receptor kinase A
  (TrkA) synapse binding regenerant which is expected to help restore the function of damaged cholinergic nerves and
  prevent degeneration, and E2025, an anti-Erythropoietin-producing hepatocellular receptor A4 (EphA4) antibody which is
  expected to suppress synaptic function decline by targeting the astrocyte pathway, are both undergoing Phase I clinical
  studies in the United States. In Japan, the Eisai-Keio Innovation Lab for Dementia (EKID), the industry-medicine
  collaborative base established jointly with Keio University, is advancing exploratory research and drug discovery research
  on a drug discovery target related to the protective mechanism inherent in a brain and the maintenance and strengthening
  of the brain’s robustness. Development on in-house developed brain disposition-oriented bispecific antibody technology
  Evolpath is also proceeding in multiple projects.
     By uniquely identifying and developing biomarkers related to the mechanisms of diseases from clinical trial data and
  real-world data, we are constructing a Brain Health Panel and advancing the redefinition of diseases based on biomarkers
  and data science. This Brain Health Panel will be utilized for the development of next-generation drugs and precision
  medicine.


(b) Dementia Ecosystem
    Through the dementia ecosystem, we aim to provide solutions ranging from maintaining good health, disease
  awareness and prevention before dementia onset in the daily living domain, to solutions for accurate diagnosis and
  confirmation of the effectiveness of treatments (drug and non-drug treatments) as well as solutions that will contribute to
  improving quality of life after dementia onset in the medical domain. In the daily living domain, Arteryex Inc., a
  subsidiary of the Company, is providing health management services (click-karte). Theoria technologies, Co., Ltd., a
  digital business company, operates the dementia portal site Theotol, providing useful dementia-related information for
  stages from high-risk through onset and treatment to prognosis, as well as conducting the development and provision of
  Sasaeru, an application that helps facilitate communication between people with dementia, doctors and caregivers in the
  treatment of dementia. EcoNaviSta, Inc., which is planned to become a wholly owned subsidiary through a successful
  takeover bid (TOB), aims to contribute to early detection of MCI and dementia and improvement of work efficiency for
  nursing care providers, through Life Rhythm Navi, a SaaS-type (cloud-based) monitoring service for the elderly.
    In Japan, by utilizing “NouKNOW”, a digital tool (non-medical device) for brain health, the Company is progressing
  with various collaborations with local governments and other industries, such as insurance, finance, automobile and food,
  to expand the dementia ecosystem. In China, we offer online medical consultations through Yin Fa Tong, a one-stop




                                                        - 15 -
                                                                               Translation for reference purpose only

  online health platform from daily life to medical treatment, and are working to reduce health care disparities through the
  use of digital technology. In Asia, we are working on expanding our ecosystem creation with other industries and non-
  profit organizations to increase dementia recognition rates and facilitate early detection and early diagnosis.


(c) Oncology Area
     Efforts to maximize the value of the anticancer agent Lenvima (jointly developed with Merck & Co., Inc., Rahway, NJ,
  USA) are progressing in the existing indications, such as monotherapy for the treatment of thyroid cancer, hepatocellular
  carcinoma and thymic carcinoma (Japan), and combination therapy with pembrolizumab for renal cell carcinoma and
  endometrial carcinoma. Furthermore, regarding the combination therapy with pembrolizumab, in addition to LEAP
  studies for hepatocellular carcinoma in combination with transcatheter arterial chemoembolization and esophageal
  carcinoma, clinical studies are underway for new combination therapy indications for renal cell carcinoma.
    In the development of next-generation oncology products, we are working to uncover drug resistance mechanisms
  utilizing biomarker data obtained from Lenvima and Halaven. Using Eisai Group’s precision chemistry technology to turn
  undruggable intracellular therapeutic targets into druggable ones, we will create new backbone therapeutic drugs.
    We are also advancing the development of MORAb-202, an antibody-drug conjugate using eribulin as the payload, and
  E7386, a first-in-class middle molecule agent expected to overcome resistance to Lenvima, as well as splicing modulators
  and protein degradation inducing agents from our drug discovery platform that embodies the Group’s strengths in
  chemistry.


(d) Global Health Area
    Along with considering efforts to resolve the global issue of access to medicines to be business envisioned under our
  Concept as well as a long-term investment for the future, the Group is promoting such undertakings proactively under
  public private partnerships with governments, international organs, private nonprofit organizations and others. For the
  elimination of lymphatic filariasis, one of the neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) endemic in developing and emerging
  countries, we are providing lymphatic filariasis treatment diethylcarbamazine (DEC) tablets to the World Health
  Organization (WHO) at price zero. These DEC tablets are manufactured at the Group’s Vizag Plant in India. The Group is
  committed to supplying DEC tablets until lymphatic filariasis is eliminated in all endemic countries that need DEC
  tablets. As of the end of March 2025, 2.52 billion tablets have been supplied to 32 countries, of which 8 countries have
  achieved lymphatic filariasis elimination. Furthermore, the Group is carrying out new drug development for NTDs such as
  mycetoma, as well as tuberculosis and malaria, under the partnership with the Japan-based Global Health Innovative
  Technology (GHIT) Fund, non-profit organizations and non-governmental organizations with extensive experience in new
  drug development related to NTDs, and academia. The Group is also working on disease awareness. Regarding
  mycetoma, a Phase IIb/III study of the antifungal agent E1224 (fosravuconazole) was conducted in Sudan by DNDi and
  the Mycetoma Research Center at the University of Khartoum. Preparations are currently under way to file for approval in
  Sudan. Regarding malaria, we have initiated a Phase I clinical study for the novel drug candidate E1018, which was
  jointly discovered with the Broad Institute in the United States.

(e) Maximization of Human Resource Value
     Eisai’s Articles of Incorporation defines employees as one of the major stakeholders and specifies that Eisai endeavors
  to “respect human rights and diversity,” “provide full opportunities for growth in support of self-fulfillment,” and “create
  an employee-friendly environment” in addition to “ensuring stable employment”. In line with this, Eisai has formulated an
  “Integrated Human Resource Strategy” and have been implementing human resource policies with the pillars of “well-
  being including employee health”, “diverse workstyle”, “employee growth”, and “organizational and business growth”,
  that ensure both individuals and the organization grow together. Respecting different opinions and values while working
  on problem-solving is a source of Eisai’s innovation and an important approach towards realizing our corporate concept.
  Eisai continues to promote the creation of a global corporate culture in which people with diverse values can play an
  active role. We also endeavor to maximize the value of human assets in the medium to long term through means such as
  conducting a Global Engagement Survey and using it to verify and enhance our human resource strategy. By publishing
  an annual “Human Capital Report” starting from FY 2023, we are disclosing initiatives and KPIs related to human capital
  linked to our human resource strategy. Based on various feedback obtained internally and externally through these
  disclosures, we are continually striving toward improving our human capital management to convert our human assets
  into genuine assets that boost our corporate value.

  (iii) Response to Tariff Policies in Various Countries
        The United States tariff measures may affect the tariff policies of other countries, raising concerns about increased
    geopolitical and economic uncertainty. In this environment, Eisai will continue to closely monitor developments in tariff
    policies in the United States and other countries, and closely examine the impact on our business. Additionally, we are
    working to establish a flexible supply chain system that takes geopolitical risks into consideration, through initiatives such as
    the establishment of multiple purchasing systems for raw materials and a manufacturing system that spans multiple factories.


  (iv) Target Financial Indicators
      In its “EWAY Future & Beyond” medium-term business plan which started in FY2021, the Group has not set any
    medium- to long-term financial targets for revenue and profits, as they are largely dependent on the social environment and
    the approval status of products under development. Instead, annual business plans are created in detail. The forecast for
    FY2025 is as follows.

4. Basic Policy for Capital Strategy
     The Group’s capital strategy revolves around “medium- to long-term Return on Equity (ROE)*1 management,” “sustainable
  and stable shareholder returns,” and “value-creative investment criteria,” which contribute to increasing shareholder value
  while ensuring financial soundness.

  (i) Medium- to Long-term ROE Management
      The Group believes that ROE is an important indicator of the sustainable creation of value for shareholders. In terms of
    medium- to long-term ROE management, the Company aims for an ROE that exceeds the cost of capital (creation of a
    positive equity spread*1) by constantly improving profit margins, financial leverage and asset turnover in the medium- to
    long-term.
       *1 Equity spread = ROE – Cost of shareholder capital


  (ii) Sustainable and Stable Shareholder Returns
        Based on factors such as a healthy balance sheet and comprehensive consideration of the consolidated financial results,
    Dividends on Equity (DOE*2), and free cash flow, as well as taking into consideration the signaling effect, the Group
    implements the stable and continuous return of profits to its shareholders. Because DOE indicates the ratio of dividends to
    consolidated net assets, the Group has positioned it as an indicator that reflects balance sheet management and,
    consequently, capital policy. Acquisition of treasury stock will be carried out appropriately after factors such as the market
    environment and capital efficiency are taken into account. The Group uses the ratio of equity attributable to owners of the
    parent and net debt equity ratio*3 as indicators to measure a healthy balance sheet.
       *2 DOE (Dividend on equity attributable to owners of the parent) = Total amount of dividends/ Total equity attributable
          to owners of the parent
       *3 Net debt equity ratio (Net DER) = {interest-bearing debt (borrowings) - cash and cash equivalents
                                          - time deposits exceeding 3 months, etc. - investment securities held by the parent}
                                              ÷ total equity attributable to owners of the parent




                                                                - 17 -
                                                                                  Translation for reference purpose only

(iii) Value-creative Investment Criteria for Growth
      To ensure that strategic investments create shareholder value, the Group invests selectively using its Value-creative
  Investment Criteria based on Net Present Value and the Internal Rate of Return spread using a risk-adjusted hurdle rate.




       Notes:
       (1) FAM: Fixed Asset Monetization
       (2) GCMS: Global Cash Management System

2. Approach and Initiatives Related to Sustainability
    The Group’s approach and initiatives related to sustainability are as follows.
    Please note that matters related to the future mentioned in the text are based on the judgment of the Group as of the end of the
  current consolidated fiscal year.


  (1) “Governance” and “Risk Management” related to Overall Sustainability
    1) Governance
        The Company has adopted a company with a nomination committee, etc. system, and has clearly separated the oversight
       of management and the execution of business. The Board of Directors delegates substantial decision-making authority for
       business execution to corporate officers, focusing instead on management oversight. Meanwhile, corporate officers strive for
       prompt, and flexible decision-making and business execution. Recognizing sustainability initiatives as a critical management
       issues that contribute to the enhancement of long-term corporate value, the Board of Directors periodic reports on the
       Group’s status of sustainability initiatives contributing to the enhancement of long-term corporate value and monitors this
       through reports on specific issues from the responsible corporate officers.
          In the operational divisions, the Sustainability Committee has been established as a body to formulate policies and
       strategies to contribute to the sustainability of society and to implement measures accordingly. The Sustainability Committee
       is co-chaired by the corporate officer in charge of company-wide business strategy and sustainability, and consists of the
       corporate officers in charge of human resources, general affairs, supply chain, risk compliance, etc. The committee examines
       ESG issues from various perspectives and implements initiatives to address them. Discussions at the Sustainability
       Committee are regularly reported to the Growth & Operating Committee, which in turn reports to the Board of Directors. In
       addition, reports on the Company’s efforts toward the sustainability agenda to the hhc Governance Committee, composed
       solely of outside directors, regarding the Company-wide initiatives related to the sustainability agenda.
          In addition, the Company has established the “Sustainability Department” dedicated to sustainability, whose mission is to
       realize a sustainable society and enhance long-term corporate value. A system has also been put in place to oversee the
       individual sustainability issues addressed by each department and the various external environments surrounding the
       Company, enabling the entire company to work together to promote initiatives. The Sustainability Department is also
       responsible for developing and implementing related initiatives to improving access to medicines in the area of Global
       Health, which the Company positions as an important contribution to sustainability.
         Further, the Company has also established the “Sustainability Advisory Board” comprised of external experts, as an
       advisory council body to the CEO. The Board aims to obtain highly qualified advice on access to medicines and variety of
       sustainability-related issues.




    2) Risk Management
         Regarding the Company’s risk management, please refer to “IV Status of the Company, 4. Status of Corporate
       Governance, etc., (1) Overview of Corporate Governance, (iii) Other Matters Related to Corporate Governance, (A) Status
       of establishment of an internal control system and a risk management system, b) Compliance risk management, ii)




                                                              - 19 -
                                                                                     Translation for reference purpose only

     Promotion of risk management.”


(2) Strategy, Indicators, and Targets for Sustainability
  (i) Eisai’s Future Creation Strategy
       In accordance with the hhc concept, Eisai is committed to realizing social good effectively by relieving anxiety over
     health and reducing health disparities, thereby increasing the satisfaction of patients and the people in the daily living
     domain and empowering them to realize their fullest life.
       In March 2025, based on the hhc concept, the Group formulated Eisai’s “Future Creation Strategy” to create a world
     where everyone can realize their fullest life. Pursuing this strategy, placed at the core of our operations, we will not only
     contribute to patients and the people in the daily living domain through the Group’s pharmaceutical related business, but also
     strengthen corporate governance and address social issues such as global environmental conservation as a foundation of our
     business. Through these efforts over the mid- to long-term, we aim to drive both long-term corporate growth and sustainable
     development of society.
       This strategy consists of four elements: (i) Time for patients and the people in the daily living domain to live their
     authentic lives, (ii) A global environment in which everyone can lead healthy lives, (iii) A society in which everyone can stay
     true to themselves, and (iv) Being a company that everyone can trust.
       In order to execute Eisai’s Future Creation Strategy and thereby enhance our mid- to long-term corporate value, the Group
     will take initiatives to identify important priority issues and targets to be addressed in our mid-term business plan.


  (ii) Initiatives to Improve Access to Medicines
       The Group considers making efforts to resolve the global issue of access to medicines to be its duty as well as a long-term
     investment for the future. It has been promoting such undertakings proactively under public-private partnerships with
     governments, international organizations, and non-profit organizations, etc.
     (A) Elimination of lymphatic filariasis (LF): LF is one of the neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) that is endemic in
         developing and emerging nations. Eisai manufactures LF medication, diethylcarbamazine (DEC) tablets, at the
         Company’s Vizag Plant in India. Until LF is eliminated in all endemic countries requiring DEC, we will continue to
         provide the tablet to the World Health Organization (WHO) free of charge. By the end of March 2025, Eisai has supplied
         2.52 billion tablets to 32 countries. Through the WHO “Global programme to eliminate lymphatic filariasis,” LF has
         been eliminated in 21 of 72 endemic countries (of the 21, Eisai has provided DEC tablets to 8 countries).
     (B) Ongoing support to NTDs: Eisai was the only Japanese company to participate in the London Declaration on Neglected
         Tropical Diseases, announced in 2012. The London Declaration is an international public-private sector partnership
         working toward the elimination of 10 NTDs. As such, we have been engaged in efforts to eliminate NTDs, including LF.
         Eisai also signed the Kigali Declaration, announced in 2022, which is the successor to the London Declaration and
         aligns with the WHO’s NTDs Roadmap 2021-2030. It aims to eliminate NTDs through enhanced ownership by endemic
         countries and further strengthened partnerships with domestic and international stakeholders. Since the London
         Declaration, activities to eliminate the diseases through global partnerships have led to the elimination of at least one
         NTD in 50 countries, and treatment interventions reaching 850 million people in 2022. However, 1.62 billion people
         worldwide are still at risk of infection by NTDs. Eisai will continue to support the elimination of NTDs through the free
         provision of DEC tablets and the development of new drugs for NTDs.
     (C) Contribution to the fight against the most neglected tropical disease, mycetoma: Since 2019, Eisai has supported and
         cooperated with the Japanese international NGO, Association for Aid and Relief, Japan (AAR Japan), which operates in
         Sudan (currently suspended due to civil war). Furthermore, Eisai conducted clinical trials for mycetoma in Sudan in
         collaboration with the Drugs for Neglected Diseases initiative (DNDi) with the in-house antifungal agent E1224
         (fosravuconazole). Preparations are currently underway for new drug approval application for E1224 in Sudan.
     (D) Development of new drugs for the treatment of NTDs and malaria: Through partnerships with international research
         agencies, etc., we are proactively undertaking the development of new drugs for the treatment of NTDs and malaria.
         Using grants from Japan’s Global Health Innovative Technology Fund (GHIT Fund) and other sources, we continue to
         develop new drugs for NTDs and malaria through partnerships that leverage the ideas of university researchers and
         nonprofit research organizations specializing in tropical diseases, such as DNDi and the Medicines for Malaria Venture
         (MMV).


  (iii) Business Activities with Consideration for the Global Environment
       Under the hhc concept, the Group considers the global environment as the foundation for business activities aimed at
     achieving social good. It is the Company’s responsibility to achieve the social good of relieving anxiety over health and




                                                             - 20 -
                                                                                   Translation for reference purpose only

  reducing health disparities, along with environmental conservation. In addition to the Company’s environmental activity
  guidelines (the “ENW Environmental Policy”), in FY2022 we established the “Eisai Environmental Management Vision”.
  This vision clearly indicates the Company’s commitment to contributing to promoting the formation of a carbon-neutral
  society by reducing greenhouse gas emissions, a recycling-oriented society through proper waste management and the
  efficient use of resources including water and a society that coexists with nature through initiatives to protect biodiversity, in
  addition to ensuring proper management of chemical substances. Amid increasing societal demands for protection of the
  global environment, the Company will fulfill its corporate social responsibility as a participant in the United Nations Global
  Compact by pursuing Company-wide efforts to reduce environmental impact in every stage of business operations, and by
  working to achieve the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) adopted by the United Nations General Assembly.
    As a result of these efforts, Eisai was selected for the highest “A List” rating in both the “CDP(*1) Climate Change Report
  2024” and the “CDP Water Security Report 2024.” We were also selected for ranking in the “Carbon Clean 200”(*2), which
  lists the top 200 publicly traded companies worldwide contributing to the reduction of fossil fuel energy consumption, for
  five consecutive years. In addition, we were included in the S&P/JPX Carbon Efficient Index. Moreover, for four
  consecutive years, we received the highest rating of “A” in the “JRECO Fluorocarbon Countermeasures Rating” of prime
  market listed companies, as well as other outside praise and recognition.
  *1: An NGO (U.K.) that requests climate change, water, and forest-related information disclosure from companies with high
      aggregate market value and municipalities, analyzes and evaluates it, and discloses it to investors, companies, and
      governments.
  *2: Selected by Corporate Knights (Canada), a media and research company, with the NGO As You Sow (U.S.).
  (A) Initiatives toward a recycling-oriented society
       The Group aims to contribute to the establishment of a recycling-oriented society through the sustainable use of
    resources, including water.
       In regard to sustainable use of water, Eisai set a medium-term target of reducing the use of water by 7% (by revenue
    intensity) by FY2030 compared to the level in FY2023 through promotion of water usage reduction via recycling and
    other efficient ways of using water resources. In addition, the Company continues to have zero legal violations by
    maintaining high-quality wastewater treatment that contributes to water quality conservation and complying with
    environmental standards related to water, in line with the medium-term target of maintaining zero legal violations until
    FY2030. Further, in FY2023, we established Internal Water Pricing (IWP), which incorporates water usage reduction into
    investment judgment criteria as the investment effect amount by converting the volume of water usage reductions into
    monetary amounts, in each country where we have major sites.
      In regard to the recycling of resources, Group companies in Japan are promoting the reduction of waste generation,
    improvements in recycling rates, and reductions in final disposal volumes. We achieved zero emissions, with the ratio of
    final disposal volume to waste generation volume being 0.5% or less for the 17th consecutive year in FY2023.


  (B) Initiatives for Biodiversity Conservation
       The Group aims to contribute to the realization of a society that coexists with nature, based on harmony with the global
    environment, through the sustainable use of biological resources and the conservation of biodiversity. We established the
    “ENW Biodiversity Guidelines” and set a medium-term target to identify and conserve important species at each business
    site by FY2030. In FY2023, we identified the following as medium- to long-term risks that the Company’s business
    activities pose to biodiversity.
    i) The impact of emissions of hazardous substances into the water and atmosphere on biodiversity in neighboring areas to
    the places where the Company operates.
    ii) The impact of waste generated from the use of the Company’s products on the environment as a whole.
       On the grounds of the Kawashima Plant (Gifu Prefecture), we maintain a Japanese garden rich with nature, and about
    700 types of medicinal and otherwise beneficial plants, including endangered species, are cultivated and managed in the
    Medicinal Herbal Garden at the Naito Museum of Pharmaceutical Science and Industry. In particular, we are engaged in
    the conservation of all 40 species listed as endangered (including near threatened species) on the Ministry of the
    Environment’s Red List. At EA Pharma Co., Ltd.’s Fukushima Plant, efforts are made to preserve the planted forest on the
    premises, which includes Somei Yoshino cherry trees and weeping cherry trees. Since FY2020, Eisai Pharmaceuticals
    India Pvt. Ltd. (India) has undertaken a tree-planting program to promote environmental awareness and have planted more
    than 10,000 trees to date. At the Bogor Plant (Indonesia), efforts are underway to conserve orchids protected by the
    national government.

         (B) Reduction of greenhouse gas emissions
              Eisai is engaged in initiatives aimed at achieving the globally-shared target of “Keeping the rise in the average global
            temperature to 1.5°C or less compared to pre-industrial levels” (the 1.5°C reduction target), as well as the achievement of
            “Net Zero* by 2050”, which is an extension of the 1.5°C target. In November 2023, the Company set an SBT 1.5°C target
            and obtained approval from the Science Based Targets initiative. In addition, in December 2023, we received approval
            from the Japan Climate Initiative (JCI) to participate in the JCI Race to Zero Circle which is committed to achieving net
            zero by 2050. Further, the Company joined the RE100 initiative, which aims to switch all electrical energy used for
            business activities to renewable energy, and we aim to achieve this target by FY2030.

            <2050 Greenhouse Gas Emissions Net Zero Targets set in FY2023>
            ● Net zero achievement target year: 2050
            ● Greenhouse gas reduction target by FY2030 (SBT1.5°C target)
             - Reduce GHG emissions (Scope 1+2) by 55% by FY2030 from base year FY2019.
             - Reduce GHG emissions (Scope 3 emissions originating from purchased products and services) by 27.5% by FY2030
               from base year FY2019.


             *: Net Zero (as defined by the Science Based Targets initiative’s Corporate Net-Zero Standard)
             • Reducing scope 1, 2 and 3 emissions to zero or to a residual level that is consistent with reaching net-zero emissions at
               the global or sector level in eligible 1.5°C -aligned pathways.
             • Neutralizing any residual emissions at net-zero target year and any GHG emissions released into the atmosphere
               thereafter.


            (Targets and performance)

  (c) Promoting the adoption of renewable energy
       The Group is expanding the adoption of renewable energy by utilizing non-fossil fuel certificates, etc. Overseas, Eisai
    has commenced full-scale operations of a large 3 MW solar power generation facility at the Vizag site (India). In addition,
    facilities such as the Exton site in the United States and the Suzhou Plant in China generate electricity through solar
    power for captive consumption. The European Knowledge Centre (U.K.) also uses green power (*1). These are some of
    the Company’s main overseas business sites where we continue to achieve 100% renewable energy usage. Through these
    initiatives, a renewable energy adoption ratio of 99.8% (*2) of the Company’s total power consumption was achieved in
    FY2023. We will continue to introduce renewable energy in the future, and strive to reduce CO2 emissions.
    *1: Electric power generated from sunlight, wind power, hydraulic power, and other renewable energy sources
    *2: Limited to externally purchased electricity

  (D) Efforts related to sales vehicles (domestic Group)
       Group companies in Japan are steadily switching sales vehicles to hybrid cars in order to reduce CO2 emissions from
    sales activities. Following FY2023, the adoption rate of hybrid vehicles reached 100% as of the end of FY2024. The
    Company also began introducing electric cars in the second half of FY2019, in an effort to reduce CO2 emissions by
    transitioning to vehicles with high fuel efficiency.


(v) Basic Approach Regarding Maximization of the Value of Human Assets
     The Company’s Articles of Incorporation define employees as important stakeholders in the achievement of its Corporate
  Concept--the hhc concept--and state that the Company will “ensure stable employment,” “respect human rights and
  diversity,” “provide full opportunities for growth in support of self-fulfillment,” and “create an employee-friendly
  environment.”
    Based on the Articles of Incorporation, in FY2024 the Company articulated a global HR Purpose to “unleash the energy of
  each individual employee, foster organizational synergy, and maximize social impact,” and everyone in the Company aims
  to contribute broadly to global society.
    In addition, as we evolve into an hhceco company that contributes broadly to everyone from patients to the people in the
  daily living domain, promoting diversity, equity, and inclusion (DE&I) is one of the Company’s most critical themes in
  striving for further achievement of social good. Since the Representative Corporate Officer and CEO announced the Eisai
  Diversity Declaration in 2012, we have worked toward the development of a culture that enables talents with diverse values
  to play active roles, regardless of their nationality, gender, age, or other characteristics. In 2023, the Company’s
  Representative Corporate Officer and CEO articulated a new DE&I message both internally and externally, and developed
  the slogan “We see difference, we see potential,” which express Eisai’s commitment to the hhc concept and DE&I and the
  subsequent statement. From FY2024 onward, we are striving to embed this slogan and statement as a shared core value
  among all global employees. As of February 2025, 80% of global employees recognize the slogan “We see difference, we
  see potential.”, and improvements have been observed in the DE&I-related items of the engagement survey. In FY2025, we
  will continue to focus on improving organizational management to reflect everyday behaviors of each employee and foster
  synergy.


  (A) Ensuring diversity globally
       The Eisai Group has appointed foreign Directors since 2002, foreign national Corporate Officers since 2006, female
    directors since 2009, and female corporate officers since 2013. As of March 31, 2025, out of 31 Corporate Officers, 4 are
    foreign nationals and 5 are women. As a global company, we are promoting the recruitment of local talent for
    management of corporations, and have assigned local talents who are Corporate Officers of the Company to management
    positions in various regions such as the Americas, EMEA, and China. At the Headquarters and across global regions and
    divisions, we continue activities to ensure employee diversity through bottom-up committee formation and promotion of
    projects tailored to the challenges experienced in each workplace. As a result, the proportion of women in management
    positions globally is approximately 37%.
      However, in Japan, compared to other countries, there remain challenges in achieving diversity, including gender
    diversity. Various measures are urgently needed to address this issue. Since the Eisai Diversity Declaration in 2012, we




                                                           - 24 -
                                                                                    Translation for reference purpose only

  have implemented, and continue to implement, reforms such as the abolition of general positions in favor of career-track
  positions, expanding recruitment of international students and mid-career hires, establishing a domestic DE&I promotion
  structure aimed at fostering an early cultural shift, and conducting various training programs, mentorship systems, and
  career counseling functions. Particularly critical is the increase in the proportion of women in management positions,
  where we focus on fostering career perspectives for women and nurturing manager candidates. Emphasizing “early,”
  “regular,” and “specific,” we create opportunities to envision leadership roles early on and implement career support
  measures for both men and women during the childcare phase. As a result, as of March 31, 2025, the proportion of women
  in management positions in Japan was 13.6% and continues to rise.
    Regarding the recruitment of foreign talent at Eisai, we have been hiring international students since FY2013 and aim
  for continuous talent acquisition. Since then, except for FY2014 and FY2017, we have consistently recruited international
  students. We are advancing the acquisition of external talents by appointing mid-career hires to management positions and
  implementing a referral hiring system. Further, in Japan, we continue to actively recruit individuals with disabilities, in
  line with statutory requirements.
    Leveraging our strengths as a global company striving to achieve the hhc concept, the Group integrates knowledge
  from regions and functions outside Japan to promote DE&I globally in a collaborative manner.

(B) Efforts toward maximization of the value of human assets
    The Company has formulated an integrated human resource strategy aiming for leaps in employee “health,”
  “workstyle,” “growth,” and “business/organization.” We actively invest in improvement of working environment to
  promote HR initiatives that unleash the energy of each employee and to create organizational synergy through the
  cultivation of leadership, DE&I, and an innovative culture, thereby aiming to maximize the value of human assets. We
  believe that maximizing the value of human assets is directly linked to maximizing social impact.


  a) Pursuit of an environment that enables employees to work in a healthy, safe, and secure manner (“Health”)
     In June 2019, Eisai issued the “Eisai Health Declaration,” prioritizing employee health as essential to achieving the hhc
  concept. We vigorously promote well-being management activities to maintain and enhance employee health. From April
  2022, as part of the Company’s second-phase strategic focus, we have set three key initiatives: enhancing health literacy,
  physical health, and mental health. Since 2023, we have conducted health literacy surveys to gauge literacy levels and
  drive various effective measures, aiming to create environments where individuals can keep living vibrantly and true to
  themselves with both physical and mental health.


  <Specific efforts>
    In collaboration with the Eisai Health Insurance Society to help maintain and improve employee health, we have
  expanded the age range for milestone comprehensive medical examinations in addition to periodic health checkups, and
  introduced predictive health check simulations based on previous health examination results from FY2024. We have
  expanded the Company’s home help support program to partially subsidize costs when employees require home helpers,
  and have expanded eligibility for survivor annuity pensions in the event of unforeseen circumstances, from the
  perspectives of both health and peace of mind.


  b) Pursuit of productivity/efficiency through achieving diverse workstyles (“Workstyle”)
     Since the concept of “Eisai Innovation (EI)” was announced in April 1990, which stated that “Eisai seeks to be the best
  vessel for all employees to actualize themselves,” we have been advancing initiatives to create a work environment where




                                                           - 25 -
                                                                               Translation for reference purpose only


each employee can thrive and to achieve workstyle which can maximize their performance. The Company’s Articles of
Incorporation explicitly state our commitment to “creating an employee-friendly environment.” Embracing the diverse
environments and values of each employee, we ensure that each employee can approach their work in a healthy, authentic,
and highly productive manner across various work environments with the idea of Work Life Bests.

<Specific efforts>
  We are advancing the creation of diverse workstyles to pursue Work Life Best, aiming for the best of both work and life
for employees, as well as productivity and efficiency. We promote various employee workstyles such as “workations” and
vacation systems for continuous leave, side-job systems aimed at self-fulfillment and career building, expanded
discretionary work systems to enhance individual autonomy, support for the participation of men in childcare, the
expansion of related systems to support balancing childcare, infertility treatment, and extension of eligibility for welfare
benefits to common-law and same-sex marriages.


c) Provision of opportunities for growth aimed at the self-fulfillment of employees (“Employee growth”)
  As an hhc company focused on patients and the people in the daily living domain, we continue to implement efforts to
understand the thoughts and feelings of patients and the people in the daily living domain. To foster the diversity needed
to meet the increasingly diverse needs of patients and the people in the daily living domain, we support employees in
autonomously continuing to grow toward realizing their future and achieving the hhc concept and self-fulfillment, and we
foster a corporate culture that praises their efforts to take on challenges.


<Specific efforts>
  The Company provides opportunities for employees to engage in “socialization” with patients and their families
through various training programs. We aim to achieve an environment that allows the dynamic growth of talents who
build trust with people inside and outside the Company, take on challenges, and continue to provide value to society.
  The keywords are autonomy and taking on challenges. Many of the training programs are held on an open-participation
basis. Each participant proactively takes necessary actions, with an eye on their own future and on the current situation.
After training, emphasis is placed on practical application, and they are expected to take on challenges, large and small.
We believe that there are lessons to be learned from taking on challenges. We are also increasing opportunities for
employees to gain cross-boundary experiences outside the Company. The involvement of other people is also important in
promoting autonomy and taking on challenges. Thus, we are encouraging one-on-one meetings between supervisors and
their team members to enable accelerated individual career formation and personal growth through work. We are engaging
in new measures, both hard and soft, including the introduction of the special paid vacation system that can be used when
carrying out self-development and social contribution activities, and holding online dialogue with both internal and
external members, as a forum for training participants to share their learning.

d) Sustainable business growth through creating a corporate culture and organizational structure that praises taking on
  challenges (“Business/Organization”)
  The Company aims to achieve social good efficiently by having each employee carry out daily work with motivation, a
sense of achievement, and a strong resonation with hhc concept. In addition, through data-backed, objective decision-
making, we aim towards evolving into a company that delivers higher social value.

<Specific efforts>
i. Improvement of employee engagement
     Central to the Company’s talent strategy is anchoring high engagement of employees to the execution of work,
  leading to high productivity and patient/customer satisfaction. The Company introduced a monthly survey of employee
  engagement in May 2020, and holds discussions and continues to make improvements in the workplace based on the
  scores. In FY2021, we introduced a global engagement survey, and are promoting contribution to customers by
  enhancing job satisfaction throughout all Group companies. In the fourth survey, which was conducted in February
  2025, a record-high 94% of employees responded globally (Reference: 90% response rate in the third survey), and 85%
  of the respondents indicated high engagement, exceeding the benchmark. We are globally promoting efforts to enhance
  employee engagement through score analysis, sharing, and implementation of actions.


ii. Optimization of organization/human resource management
      Since FY2024, we have been expanding the scope of data utilization and are continuing to promote real-time people
    analytics (collection and analysis of data related to employees and the organization). To this end, we are developing a



                                                       - 26 -
                                                                                   Translation for reference purpose only

       data mart designed to enable the effective use of diverse HR data. This data mart will ensure that HR information
       systems such as kaonavi are constantly updated, enabling real-time access to information for organizational and human
       asset management and contributing to further optimization.
         Further, for critical global positions, annual succession planning processes ensure diversity in selecting, developing,
       and promoting next-generation leaders across nationalities, sex, and age groups.

(vi) Human Rights Initiatives
     The Group recognizes human rights as the most universal and fundamental requirement in business operations. We
  promote efforts to respect human rights aligned with international standards such as the UN Guiding Principles on Business
  and Human Rights, OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises on Responsible Business Conduct, and the Tripartite
  Declaration of Principles concerning Multinational Enterprises and Social Policy (ILO Tripartite Declaration). In March
  2019, with approval from the Company’s Executive Committee, the highest decision-making body of business execution,
  and endorsement from the Company’s Board of Directors, the “ENW Human Rights Policy” was formulated. The
  Company’s organization-wide “Business and Human Rights Project” undertakes initiatives based on international human
  rights standards. As part of the human rights due diligence conducted under this project, in FY2024, a questionnaire survey
  was conducted targeting sales subsidiaries, plants/research laboratories, and direct business partners to identify industries
  and countries/regions with high human rights risks. From FY2025 onward, we will continue implementing a human rights
  due diligence cycle that identifies risks of potential adverse impact on human rights, implements measures to avoid or
  mitigate these risks, monitors the implementation status, and discloses the results. In addition, as part of our remedies, we
  launched a grievance mechanism (in Japanese) in November 2024.

  (A) Efforts related to human rights in the supply chain
      As the legislation regarding initiatives in each country progresses, the Japanese government also issued the “Guidelines
    on Respecting Human Rights in Responsible Supply Chains” as its framework for addressing human rights issues in
    September 2022. As global business expands, Japanese companies are increasingly called upon to mitigate human rights
    risks within their supply chains and business partners. The Group recognizes “human rights in the supply chain” as a
    priority issue and actively engages in sustainable procurement targeting both primary suppliers of direct materials and
    secondary suppliers (where primary suppliers are trading companies or wholesalers) within and outside Japan. Through
    sustainability assessments encompassing human rights and labor standards, we strive to understand our business partners’
    efforts toward respecting human rights, identify human rights issues, and enhance engagement with them. In June and
    October 2024, we held business partner briefings for a total of 177 suppliers, which included a lecture by outside experts
    titled “The Importance of Business and Human Rights and Sustainable Procurement,” aimed at raising awareness about
    the importance of corporate responsibility to respect human rights.

  (B) Efforts to respect the human rights of employees, patients, and clinical trial participants
      In clinical research, the Company adheres to ICH-GCP standards to respect the human rights and to ensure safety of
    participants in clinical trials, obtaining informed consent based on the individual’s free will. We conduct clinical trials in
    compliance with regulatory requirements in each country, internal standards, and clinical trial plans. In addition, in order
    to provide comprehensive and equitable treatment options, Eisai positions efforts to enhance diversity in clinical trials as a
    core commitment, ensuring expanded access to clinical trials for all patients regardless of ethnicity, race, sex, age,
    socioeconomic status, gender identity, residence, or physical abilities.
       For efforts related to patients’ human rights, please refer to “(ii) Initiatives to Improve Access to Medicines.” For efforts
    related to employees’ human rights, please refer to “(v) Basic Approach Regarding Maximization of the Value of Human
    Assets.”

  (C) Human rights education and training
       To promote business activities based on respect for human rights, we conduct various activities such as human rights
    awareness training for officers and employees of the Company and domestic Group companies. In FY2024, we held a
    lecture by outside experts for corporate officers within and outside Japan on “Initiatives to Respect Human Rights in Line
    with International Standards.” In Japan, under the theme “Creating a Workplace Where All Employees Can Thrive (For
    the Achievement of Social Good),” we conducted human rights awareness training focused particularly on LGBTQ+
    matters, with a participation of 4,772 employees, (participation rate: 93%,) including domestic Group companies.

(vii) Sustainable Procurement
      Companies are required to engage in sustainable procurement activities that prioritize human rights, labor and safety, the
  environment, and ethics throughout the entire supply chain. Sustainable procurement prevents the occurrence of human
  rights violations as well as issues related to the environment in the supply chain. It also enables the building of a robust and
  sustainable supply chain. In recognition of the efficiency and effectiveness of promoting such initiatives across the industry,
  the Company has been a member of the Pharmaceutical Supply Chain Initiative (PSCI), a global non-profit organization in
  the pharmaceutical and healthcare sector, since 2021. While adhering to PSCI principles, we are promoting sustainable
  procurement in collaboration with our business partners.
    As part of our efforts to encourage our business partners, we continue to engage by explaining our sustainability policy,
  requesting the submission of consent for the “Eisai Global Code of Conduct for Business Partners” (hereinafter, “Code of
  Conduct”) formulated in accordance with PSCI principles, conduct sustainability evaluations of our business partners using
  the EcoVadis SAS (France) platform, and provide feedback and engagement based on the evaluation results. In addition, in
  September 2022, we established the “Eisai Global Procurement Standard,” which specify compliance requirements for all
  procurement activities within the Group, and we are continuously working to enhance its effectiveness.
    In regard to direct materials, we expanded the scope of sustainable procurement to include business partners at our
  overseas plants since FY2022. By FY2023, we initiated efforts with major business partners at 3 plants in China, India, and
  Indonesia, including obtaining their consent to the Code of Conduct and sharing EcoVadis sustainability assessment results.
  Moreover, since FY2023, we included business partners related to Leqembi manufacturing in the United States in our
  sustainable procurement efforts. Direct material business partners at plants in Japan, we held the 6th and 7th business partner
  briefings remotely in FY2024, with a total of 177 suppliers participating. In addition, we engaged in dialogue based on
  sustainability assessment results with our business partners in Japan and overseas to share priority improvement points. For
  indirect materials, we request submission of consent to the Code of Conduct based on the scale of transactions.

  (E) Promotions that follow compliance
       The Eisai Group conducts promotional activities globally, in accordance with compliance. Further, in order to gain the
     broad understanding of society that our corporate activities are conducted according to the highest ethical standards, we
     disclose payments to medical institutions, etc., and patient groups in accordance with laws and regulations and guidelines
     stipulated by the Japan Pharmaceutical Manufacturers Association (JPMA) and each country.


       a) Stipulation of the Code of Conduct in the Compliance Handbook
          The Group has established guidelines for promoting actions in compliance in the “Compliance Handbook”
       distributed to all employees. The following is an excerpt.

       • Eisai markets and promotes its pharmaceutical products worldwide. ENW provides accurate and balanced scientific
        information on products to Healthcare Providers (HCPs) only for the uses for which they have been approved by the
        applicable regulatory authorities.
       • “Promotion” means any activity undertaken, organized, or sponsored by a pharmaceutical company which is directed
         at HCPs to promote the prescription, recommendation, supply, administration, or consumption of its pharmaceutical
         products through all methods of communication, including the Internet.
       • When engaging in promotional activities with HCPs, we are expected to be familiar with local laws and regulations
        for such engagements in our home country.




                                                          - 28 -
                                                                                    Translation for reference purpose only


       • Promotion in a manner not consistent with the approved label is prohibited and promotion of drugs prior to approval
         is also prohibited. All promotional materials must be reviewed and approved in accordance with local processes and
        may be used only for the approved purpose.

       b) Establishment of the Eisai Co., Ltd. Code of Practice
          In March 2012, the International Federation of Pharmaceutical Manufacturers and Associations (IFPMA) announced
       the “IFPMA Code of Practice” targeting not only marketing activities but also interactions with healthcare
       professionals, healthcare institutions, patient groups, and the promotion of pharmaceuticals. The Japan Pharmaceutical
       Manufacturers Association (JPMA), of which the Company is a member, formulated and implemented the “JPMA Code
       of Practice” in accordance with the “IFPMA Code of Practice.” The Company has established the “Eisai Co., Ltd. Code
       of Practice” in accordance with the spirit of the “JPMA Code of Practice.” All officers and employees of the Group
       adhere to this Code, ensuring high ethics and transparency at all times, fulfilling accountability for interactions with
       researchers, healthcare professionals, patient groups, etc., and striving to earn societal trust through their activities.


(ix) Efforts Related to Information Security
     While the use of IT and digital technologies is advancing, cyber attacks are becoming increasingly sophisticated and
  complex, including ransomware and targeted email attacks, thereby increasing the risk of operational shutdowns and other
  disruptions to business activities.
     The Group holds substantial volume of critical information assets, including personal information, undisclosed
  information, and confidential information shared with partner companies. A leak, falsification, or loss of such important
  information could result in legal liabilities, loss of competitive advantage, and ultimately a loss of trust in the Company. In
  particular, accurate compliance with global regulations on personal information protection is essential, and the leakage of
  unpublished structural formulas for projects in the drug discovery phase could adversely affect the processes for filing and
  obtaining patents.
    To address such risks, under the leadership of the corporate officer responsible for internal control and the Chief
  Information Officer, the Group is implementing multilayered information security measures as described below, and is
  working to continuously enhance governance related to global information security and implement related measures.
  • Enhancing the global information security governance system
  • Strengthening system infrastructure
  • Established regulations and other guidelines related to information management and regular review
  • Provided officers and employees with information security education and training opportunities (e.g., targeted email attack
    simulations, e-learning)
  • Establishing an incident response system (CSIRT)

(x) Efforts Toward Investment in Intellectual Property
     The legal protection and effective utilization of the proprietary technologies and products the Group has developed
  through our investments in R&D and business activities are indispensable to the sustainable growth and development of the
  Company and the stable delivery of pharmaceuticals to patients. For this reason, we are strategically pursuing various
  activities related to intellectual property, such as patent acquisition.
   For more details on the Group’s intellectual property investment efforts, please refer to “II Status of Business, 1.
  Management Policies, Management Environment, and Issues to Address (3) Management environment, management
  policies and strategies, and priority issues to address in business and financial fields, etc., (i) Medium-term Business Plan
  ‘EWAY Future & Beyond,’ (ii) Progress and Efforts of the Medium-term Business Plan ‘EWAY Future & Beyond,’” and our
  website at https://www.eisai.com/innovation/research/policies_guidelines/ip/index.html.

3. Risk Factors
          The following is a list of major risks and uncertainties that management recognizes as having the potential to significantly
     affect the financial position, financial results, and cash flows of the Group, among other matters related to the business and
     accounting conditions described in the Annual Securities Report. However, these do not cover all of the risks and uncertainties
     faced by the Group, and it is possible that they will be affected in the future by other factors that cannot be foreseen, or are not
     deemed to be important, at this point in time.
       The risks and uncertainties surrounding the Group are regularly discussed by the Growth & Operating Committee and other
     decision-making bodies, and we consider ways to take advantage of them as opportunities or reduce these risks and
     uncertainties. The results of these discussions are reported to and discussed by the Board of Directors, and the risks and
     uncertainties described below reflect discussions not only on the executive side but also at Board meetings.
       These are judgments as of the end of the current consolidated fiscal year, and statements in the text regarding the future are
     not guarantees that they will occur or be achieved.


   1) Corporate Concept

Management            The Company has redefined the key players in our corporate concept of human health care (hhc) as “people in
based on            the daily living and medical domains” and expanded the key players we should contribute to from “patients and
the Corporate       their families” to “patients and the people in the daily living domain.” In June 2022, the Articles of Incorporation
Concept             were partially amended to stipulate that the Company’s corporate concept is to give first thought to patients and
                    the people in the daily living domain, and to increase the benefits that health care provides to them. These aims
                    are shared with stakeholders and considered as our “Purpose.” We also believe that the increased benefit to
                    patients and the people in the daily living domain resulting from achievement of these aims will lead to
                    improved performance of the Group and increased corporate value in the long term. The strategic intent of the
                    medium-term business plan “EWAY Future & Beyond,” which started in April 2021, and the establishment of a
                    business model that promotes collaboration with other industries in the hhceco (hhc concept + ecosystem)
                    Declaration issued in May 2022, are also based on the hhc corporate concept. As a company that seeks to
                    effectively achieve social good in the form of relieving anxiety over health and reducing health disparities, our
                    strong motivation based on our understanding of the true needs of patients becomes the source of the Group’s
                    innovation. In addition, we view the importance of promoting the information management/provision, etc.,
                    needed to promote further the research and development of new drugs, produce and sell high-quality products,
                    and achieve safe use of pharmaceuticals, on a foundation of controls, aimed at creating patient value, as
                    “Integrity.” This concept is also the building block of our ESG efforts, such as activities for improving access to
                    medicines including free provision of a lymphatic filariasis treatment, and building of a community that coexists
                    with dementia.
                      Accordingly, insufficient permeation of the corporate concept throughout the Group, stagnation of the
                    implementation of management aimed at implementing the concept, and other factors that hinder the full
                    increase of benefit to patients and the people in the daily living domain may have significant impact not only on
                    the Group’s business performance, but also on the improvement of corporate value, including non-financial
                    value.


   2) Business Strategy

Maximizing            The Group has determined that maximizing the value of next-generation Alzheimer’s disease (AD) treatments
the value of        including the AD treatment Leqembi (generic name: lecanemab) is one of the most important strategies in the
Leqembi and         medium-term business plan “EWAY Future & Beyond.” As part of that, we are building diagnostic and treatment
next-               pathways that lead from the start of the patient’s examination through to their diagnosis, treatment and
generation AD       monitoring. Regarding Leqembi, we are aiming to improve the usability of these pathways along with
treatments          advancements in blood biomarkers and the development of maintenance treatment and subcutaneous injection
                    formulations. If these cannot be completed, next-generation AD treatments might not sufficiently reach patients

                    explanations based on the concept of value-based pricing. However, if patients’ access to Leqembi is limited by

Maximizing         The Group and Merck & Co., Inc., Rahway, NJ, USA are conducting multiple clinical trials for the combined
the value of     treatment of the anticancer agent Lenvima and other treatments, including the anti-PD-1 antibody
